Routa now uses Oxlint and Oxfmt throughout repository tooling

Biome dependencies, configs and development commands are removed from the root, docs, design, and examples. CI runs the same Vite Plus commands. Generated starter projects keep their original Biome and Vitest defaults.

Vite Plus 1.3.0. Apple M4 Pro, 12 logical CPUs, 24 GiB RAM, Node 24.18.0. Migration PR #151.

One toolchain, with a measured cost

The repository now uses vp check, vp lint, and vp fmt. Dependency declarations and secret checks run through Oxlint JavaScript plugins, which add launch and execution overhead. The matched lint timings below include those checks. This migration does not provide a general speedup.

Oxfmt covers Markdown, MDX, CSS, YAML, JSON, HTML and Astro. The pinned Astro formatter drops frontmatter comments, so frontmatter formatting is disabled to preserve them. Oxlint still checks script blocks, and explicit Astro checks remain required. Script rules, import organization, template and CSS lint coverage differ from Biome; the audit below lists the remaining gaps.

After review: restored checks and measured costs

Six supported Biome checks are restored explicitly: useless constructors, wrapper types, async functions without await, template strings, optional chains, and accumulating spreads. Targeted negative probes trigger all six. Compiler use-before-declaration checks are partial; broad Function/empty-object bans, JSX blank-target checks, test nesting limits, and automatic import organization remain different. Import sorting is explicitly disabled to preserve authored initialization order.

Explicit Biome rule audit and deliberate gaps
Former rule Replacement Limits and decision
noInvalidUseBeforeDeclaration, error Compiler diagnostics in vp lint and explicit TypeScript checks Partial: compiler checks invalid lexical uses, not the same lint semantics. Oxlint's broader no-use-before-define is deliberately off because hoisted functions and forward type references are valid in this codebase.
noUndeclaredDependencies, error dependency-checks/no-extraneous-dependencies, error Runtime package source only, excluding tests. Plugin semantics differ from Biome; packed consumer checks remain required.
noUnusedImports, error no-unused-vars, error Includes import bindings; no automatic import removal on a check.
noUnusedVariables, error no-unused-vars, error Naming and type-only-use semantics differ.
useImportExtensions, off No extension lint rule enabled Preserve TypeScript/ESM build handling.
useJsonImportAttributes, off No matching lint rule enabled Preserve compiler/runtime checks.
noConsole, warn no-console, warn Tests exempt; same intentional console usage remains advisory.
noDebugger, error no-debugger, error Explicitly enabled.
noExplicitAny, off typescript/no-explicit-any off No new ban on explicit any.
noFocusedTests, error vitest/no-focused-tests, error Recognizes bundled vite-plus/test.
noSkippedTests, error vitest/no-disabled-tests, error Test runner semantics differ; focused/skipped checks are explicit.
noTsIgnore, error typescript/ban-ts-comment, error Rejects ts-ignore and ts-nocheck; allows intentional ts-expect-error, whose use is compiler-checked.
useAwait, warn typescript/require-await, warn Type-aware: allows returning a promise without await; not identical to Biome's syntax rule.
noDefaultExport, off No default-export ban Vite/Astro configuration uses default exports.
noInferrableTypes, off No inferrable-type restriction enabled No added style restriction.
noMagicNumbers, off No magic-number restriction enabled No added style restriction.
useExportType, warn typescript/consistent-type-exports, warn Type-aware counterpart.
useImportType, error typescript/consistent-type-imports, error Inline type import fixes.
useNodejsImportProtocol, error unicorn/prefer-node-protocol, error Explicitly enabled.
useTemplate, warn prefer-template, warn Explicitly enabled; fix semantics differ.
noBannedTypes, error typescript/no-wrapper-object-types, error Partial: primitive wrappers are rejected; Biome's bans on broad Function and {} types are not reproduced. The framework deliberately uses structurally empty extension contracts.
noUselessConstructor, error no-useless-constructor, error Explicitly enabled.
useOptionalChain, warn typescript/prefer-optional-chain, warn Type-aware counterpart; may select different safe rewrites.
noBlankTarget, error No equivalent enabled Gap: no JSX rule/plugin added for a repository with no JSX components. Astro template safety is not covered by script lint; Astro checks/builds do not establish this rule's equivalence.
noGlobalEval, error no-eval, error Explicitly enabled.
noSecrets, error no-secrets/no-secrets, error JavaScript plugin with tolerance 4.5; heuristics differ. Tests exempt.
noAccumulatingSpread, warn oxc/no-accumulating-spread, warn Explicitly enabled.

Fresh matched measurements on 2026-10-23 use the same 28-file seed, alternating before/after pairs, and a discarded warmup. Both sides use vp. These 28 measured commands passed. The syntax tier omits type-aware-only rules; the typed tier includes them. Checks differ in semantics, so timings compare the stated configurations, not identical diagnostics.

Workload Original tools via vp After review fixes Change
Syntax lint: identical bytes7 runs per side 111 ms109 to 112 ms 353 ms351 to 371 ms +218.3%
Lint plus type diagnostics on identical source7 runs per side 477 ms471 to 491 ms 703 ms675 to 746 ms +47.4%

Syntax lint: identical bytes: median peak RSS 65 → 209 MiB; CPU 0.23 → 0.59 s. Lint plus type diagnostics on identical source: median peak RSS 259 → 362 MiB; CPU 1.41 → 1.73 s.

The pristine OpenAPI scaffold preview previously reported two conflicts after formatting. Restored files now match all four manifest entries and the preview shows every entry unchanged. The formatter excludes this fixture, and the normal verification gate checks its hashes. Generator templates and their original consumer-tool defaults are preserved.

Dependency baseline: 619 → 812 lockfile package entries, an increase of 193 entries, 31.2%. This counts package entries, not installation bytes. Tooling log outputs now live under gitignored .artifacts/tooling/, separate from Dex. Those logs are local rerun outputs, not portable evidence links; commands, outcomes and individual samples are embedded below.

Verification and scope

All 204 tests in nine files pass. Both packed starter variants install and run, and the packaged HTTP server returns the expected response. All 26 generated scaffold files retain their original hashes. Eleven targeted probes verify type errors, unawaited promises, focused tests, formatting, undeclared runtime dependencies, secret detection and Astro comment preservation.

Clean lint jobs build workspace declarations and run Astro sync before type-aware analysis. This fixes missing workspace declarations and Astro virtual-module types in fresh CI checkouts.

The current root has 8 direct development dependencies; lockfile package entries increased from 619 to 812, an increase of 193 entries or 31.2%. Biome has no entry in the workspace lockfile. Oxlint's plugin APIs and compiler diagnostics remain experimental; dependency and secret checks are tested replacements, not a claim of identical detection.

Pairs run serially in alternating before/after order. Both sides use vp. The recorded pre-review gate covered 239 formatted files and 96 linted files, including apps and examples; legacy coverage differs.

Earlier hybrid candidate: historical measurements

The following 128 samples measured the previous candidate while it still retained Biome. Its recommendation, dependency footprint, and full-workflow timings describe that earlier state. The five measurements above supersede the overlapping cases.

Vite Plus: the measured tradeoffs for Routa

The branch now uses vp for development commands, verification, package operations, and CI. Here are the repeated numbers for choosing which tools to keep.

22 October 2026. Apple M4 Pro, 12 logical CPUs, 24 GiB RAM. Node 24.18.0 on both test runners. Vite Plus 1.3.0. Original commit f07c761.

My recommendation: keep Biome if speed is the reason to switch

Formatting and syntax lint are faster with Biome on this repository. Vite Task saves about 40 ms on a warm build lookup, while vp pack adds about 120 ms to an uncached package build. The full gate with cold task caches changes by +0.12 s, or +0.6%, in three runs per side. These samples do not establish a general speed advantage.

I would keep the vp command interface, preserve the test-coverage fix, and retain Biome formatting and syntax lint for performance. Broader adoption should be a deliberate choice for coordinated tooling and type-aware rules, with the memory and maintenance tradeoffs below. The branch remains the measured migration candidate.

The warm full-gate comparison does different work. Turbo replays all three test packages and its type checks. The VP branch executes all 204 tests and explicit type checks every time. Its build outputs are cached. The cold-task comparison clears both task caches before every sample, so both sides execute the tests. The workspace-build warm row has a separate policy difference: this candidate leaves Astro and example build scripts uncached. That is a configurable choice, not a Vite Task limitation.

The explicit typecheck row expands coverage. Original configs include 1 of 9 test files; corrected configs include all 9. This fix is useful independently of Vite Plus. The matched typed tier above holds that coverage constant.

Correctness remains part of the decision

All 128 measured invocations exit successfully. All 26 scaffold files keep their original hashes. Docs and design retain Astro; generated projects retain their original Biome and Vitest settings.

The cache regression test caught a stale bundled CLI after a creator change. Explicit dependency-output inputs now fix it. Four cache scenarios pass, including all 19 output files restored with matching bytes and modes.

Method, exact commands, and comparison limits

Commands run serially in alternating before/after order. Small workloads have seven measured runs per side; workflows have three. One warmup per side is excluded. Task caches are explicitly cleared for cold-task tests; dependencies and the OS file cache stay warm. This is one machine and a small sample, not a general benchmark.

The legacy checkout keeps its original source, Biome, Turbo, TypeScript, Vitest, and esbuild builder. Its command orchestration is translated to VP, as requested. Node links and an exact locked native binding repair the isolated environment; the full command patch and environment adjustments are in the JSON evidence. To reproduce, create an isolated f07c761 checkout, apply baselineHarnessPatch from that JSON, install with vp install, reproduce its baselineEnvironmentRepairs, then run vp exec node scripts/benchmark-tooling.mjs --baseline /path/to/baseline from this branch. The matched source tier uses identical file bytes, with the existing Biome suppression comments restored for both tools. Format checks normalize the same source seed with each formatter first; format writes restore identical indentation before every sample.

Functional changes and before/after evidence

System installation

Adopted

Before

vp 0.2.2 was installed through Bun. Node and pnpm were managed separately.

After

vp 1.3.0 is installed through the official system installer. The repository also pins vite-plus@1.3.0, so CI and contributors use the same local release.

Evidence. The environment doctor passes. Existing fnm Node 24.18.0 and pnpm 11.9.0 remain in system-first mode. A frozen-lockfile installation passes. The global vp command resolves the pinned local toolchain for repository commands.

Commands and technical details 
vp env doctor
vp env current --json
vp install --frozen-lockfile
vp toolchain --json

Lint, formatting, and type errors together

Adopted with Biome checks

Before

vp run check ran Biome. Disposable source/test type errors, an unused @ts-expect-error, and a floating promise passed that command. The separate verification workflow still checked source types.

After

vp run check runs vp check with type-aware lint and compiler diagnostics, then the remaining Biome checks. All four failure fixtures now fail. Focused tests fail before and after.

Evidence. All 17 check probes behave as expected. Oxfmt rejects a formatting fixture, fixes it, and accepts the result. Package-local lint also rejects floating promises. Biome still rejects an undeclared dependency and its documented fake AWS key.

Commands and technical details

Oxlint compiler diagnostics are experimental. We keep explicit TypeScript source and test checks, Astro checks, and Biome dependency/secret rules. Docs, design, and examples retain Biome formatting and linting. 

vp run check
vp run typecheck
vp run --filter @routa-ts/core lint
vp run format

The test files now participate in type checking

Fixed coverage gap

Before

Core and CLI test configs inherited an exclusion for their own tests. Only the creator package's test file was included: 1 of 9 files.

After

Both configs explicitly clear the exclusion: 9 of 9 files. The tests now await 142 CLI calls and use explicit route-context factories where context is required.

Evidence. Activating those checks exposed a real route-root typing gap. Unsupported method keys could pass through an intermediate object. The generic constraint now rejects them in both registered and explicit-context factories. Regression assertions cover objects with supported and unsupported methods together.

Commands and technical details
Test files included in explicit TypeScript programs
Package Before After Runtime tests
core 0 5 71
cli 0 3 121
create-routa-ts 1 1 12

The existing runtime assertions remain. Intentional invalid runtime fixtures use specific compiler-error assertions. A patch changeset records the public type correction.

Vitest through the pinned Vite Plus toolchain

Adopted

Before

Vitest 5.0.3 ran through Turbo. A forced test run included builds for all seven workspace packages and apps, even the sites without tests.

After

Tests import vite-plus/test and run bundled Vitest 5.0.5. Three framework test packages execute; site and example builds are outside the test chain.

Evidence. 204 tests pass in 9 files. Tests always execute. Two fixtures now declare their pnpm user-agent explicitly instead of depending on the task runner's environment. Starter-project Vitest defaults stay unchanged.

Commands and technical details 
vp run test

Use the repeated test-workflow measurements above. All tests execute on both sides in that comparison.

Library builds with explicit published entry points

Adopted with a timing tradeoff

Before

A 141-line custom script ran TypeScript, deleted maps, bundled selected files with esbuild, and checked required outputs.

After

Three package configs use vp pack. Explicit entries preserve .js exports, the CLI's spawned runtime.js, external runtime dependencies, and declarations. The custom builder and direct esbuild dependency are removed.

Evidence. Installed tarball consumers pass for both starter variants, including an HTTP request to the packaged server. The direct uncached package build is slower in this sample. Adoption reduces custom build code; it is not a cold-build speedup.

Cache package compilation and execute validation

Adopted

Before

Turbo cached builds, type checks, and tests. Cold-cache and warm-cache results are now measured separately above.

After

Vite Task replaces Turbo. Three package tasks cache their own outputs with explicit source/config/manifest/lockfile inputs. The CLI also fingerprints core and creator outputs. Tests and other package scripts are uncached.

Evidence. All four cache scenarios pass: warm hits, deleted-output restoration, propagation of creator changes into the bundled CLI, and restoration of the original source/output hashes. Restoration reproduces all 19 files byte for byte, including executable modes.

Commands and technical details 
vp run build:packages
vp run --no-cache compile

The cache is deliberately local. A dependency-order edge alone did not invalidate a bundled CLI. The passing regression probe requires creator changes to change the CLI bundle, then restores all original bytes.

Astro integration without a global Vite alias

Existing Vite retained

Before

Docs and design used Astro with regular Vite 8.3.4 and Tailwind. Both site builds already worked.

After

Astro and Tailwind continue to use their original Vite distribution. Framework tooling uses Vite Plus's bundled Vite 8.3.4. A workspace-wide alias was tried and removed after plugin-type errors.

Evidence. Both Astro checks pass with zero errors. The full build creates 32 docs pages, 14 design pages, and both examples with 5 and 11 route files. Existing i18n/404 content warnings remain.

Commands and technical details 
vp run typecheck
vp run build

Routa's API runtime, CLI TypeScript analysis, tsx development loader, pnpm, and Changesets remain in place. Vite Plus development tooling adds no Routa runtime dependency.

Generated applications and published consumers

Defaults retained

Before

Scaffolds emitted TypeScript, Biome, and Vitest tooling with the existing scripts and dependency ranges. Both OpenAPI and non-OpenAPI starters worked.

After

All 26 generated files match the baseline SHA-256 hashes. Fifteen files in the OpenAPI starter and eleven without OpenAPI are unchanged. Scaffold Vitest stays ^4.1.9; Biome stays ^2.5.2.

Evidence. Packed installation, route checking, building, unit testing, and OpenAPI validation pass. The packaged server responds with HTTP 200 and { "ok": true }. Committed example metadata stays unchanged.

Commands and technical details 
vp run generated:check
vp run pack:check

The JSON evidence includes the before/after hash for every generated file and the emitted file lists for every published package.
